#### Problems, limits and lessons: P2

**Historical full-suite finding / pre-edit Configuration impact addendum (superseded by fix round 1):** the once-only run finished `1 failed, 3801 passed, 42 skipped, 4 warnings in 980.88s`. `test_real_loader_examples` exposed that the explicit grouping value in the deployment example was rejected by `_overlay`'s top-level allowlist. The initial repair admitted only `observe.near_duplicate_cosine_distance` and passed the affected checks. Sonnet P2-1 correctly identified this as contract drift; PM withdrew that admission and instructed removal of the redundant example leaf instead. Fix round 1 below restores the existing contract. The historical full-run exit remains nonzero; the reviewer subsequently supplied a separate passing full gate, and this fix round runs only its authorized focused gate.

- Implementation and permitted verification/cleanup are finished. The full run was nonzero; its sole defect is repaired and all 480 affected checks pass, but a fresh full-suite acceptance result remains outstanding under the once-only limit. No known failing final focused case or surviving mutant remains. Independent review/integration and the outstanding full gate belong to the PM; no self-acceptance or Git write.
- Single-link chains, quadratic grouping CPU, sentinel fitting, real-model injection resistance/quality and Linux CI remain explicitly unmeasured. The new context column makes old draft observation tables incompatible until rebuilt. A crash after an external response but before durable commit may require another call; committed bundles do not.
- Repeated lesson from P1: cached-capability doubles must state both proof and remaining allowance. Omitting the latter made a fallback result empty; the test now marks the already-started fallback with allowance zero. Copy the complete fixture ownership contract, not just `ProbeResult`.
- Windows harness lesson: replacing `subprocess.Popen` with a function breaks `asyncio.windows_utils.Popen` subclassing (`<python-stdlib>/asyncio/windows_utils.py:125`, observed collector traceback). Keep a class adapter when forcing hidden children before importing asyncio. The initial collection error is not a mutant survivor or a test pass.
- Parser exception lesson: the reused canonical JSON helper raises `StorageError` for nonfinite values; translate invalid reply data to the parser's validation error before the per-image handler. Schema cache tests should use the real stat-keyed `_cached_schema` behavior, not invent `_schema.cache_clear`.

Lessons learned: the first two old-coverage invocations exited 0 but inherited Windows child-output redirection left empty log files. Capture pytest output in the actual child, pass the check name through the limiter, and rerun only the checks lacking evidence; the table contains those captured reruns. Initial cleanup attempted to delete its own open stderr file and got `WinError 32`; exclude the active output handles, then clean them after process exit. The corrected cleanup succeeded on the next attempt. Neither harness issue is presented as a production defect or mutant survivor.

Other keys follow the replacement. Refuse chains/ambiguities/normalized target collisions, never merge; unmatched sources remain lint. Removal/change reconstructs raw naming. No physical folder rename. Decision 131 subsequently adds the per-folder selector below.
2. Q2 accepted: distinct clear/partial image support; multiple semantic groups -> varies; one visible image -> single_observation. Stable requires the configured minimum, other-facet/view variation and independent near-duplicate diversity. Low diversity ratio is distinct near-duplicate groups / visible images <= required finite [0,1] `cards.low_diversity_max_group_ratio`, examples `fit_from_gt`. Other insufficiency -> stable_low_diversity plus reason lint. Zero-visible facets assert no value and emit visibility-gap lint. Required policy version is `invariance_v0`; fit nothing and document the broadened low-diversity label.
3. Q3 accepted: required CLI `--folder-count`, `--card-count`, `--seed`; nonnegative counts, zero skips, cap to sorted populations and sample without replacement with an isolated seeded RNG. JSON includes pinned snapshot, seed, requested/actual counts and sampled folders/cards. No model/source read; missing store returns explicit missing result and creates nothing.

### Step 0 addendum (decision 131)

Decision 130 accepts the P3 answers above. Decision 131 authorizes implementation without another approval gate. Research: `storage/identity.py:40-46` admits nonempty relative forward-slash paths byte for byte, refusing backslashes, absolute paths and empty/dot/parent segments. `k0_scan.py:247-257` selects immediate child directories by their original names; `k0.py:149-159` keeps that folder ID. No Unicode/case normalization or filesystem alias lookup will be added for selectors. Concept IDs still use NFKC/casefold (`storage/identity.py:16-24`).

Use a structurally disjoint schema alternative: `{concepts: {old_name: {...}}, folders: [{folder_id: relative_path, name: corrected_name}]}`, requiring both sections. The existing concept-name map stays valid, including concepts literally named `concepts` or `folders`; the new array-valued section cannot be a legacy override value. Duplicate identical folder entries are idempotent; conflicting names for one exact path are refused. Unknown paths produce lint and create nothing. A folder selector wins over the source-concept `name` for that folder only; source-concept curation follows its resulting memberships. A nameless known folder gains one membership with null description provenance. A selector on a folder with several raw concepts is refused as ambiguous, since it names no source membership; it must not silently collapse concepts. Normalized targets belonging to different raw concepts/folders are refused. Names have the same single-name/separator checks as concept selectors. `folder_override` records the new name source in tables, report and applied checkpoint. Raw naming checkpoints remain untouched; removing/changing selectors recomputes the applied view.

**Configuration impact (before decision-131 runtime edits):** no profile leaf, model role, provider/device/inference/timeout/concurrency/lifecycle setting changes. `knowledge.overrides_file` and its existing contained-source reader/hash remain the only operational input. New contract selectors, exact-path validation, precedence, ambiguity/collision refusals, provenance enum and unmatched-path lint are protocol/artifact/safety-bound, not fitted choices. Cover each in the addendum audit with parser properties, effect/refusal tests and raw-cache/hash lifecycle checks; no new sentinels/defaults or deployment overlay admission.

Plan: (1) in progress: extend schema/parser, apply per-folder names from raw rows, and preserve curation/provenance/checkpoints; (2) update contracts/K2 docs and focused properties/refusals/lifecycle tests; (3) run focused gate, 3-5 scratch mutants, audit/static/cleanup and update Completion/Handoff/Worker completion. Finish: focused tests pass, no unexplained mutant survives, every changed behavior has an audit row and cited evidence. PM owns the final-hash full integration suite; do not repeat it here.

| What does the loader already validate? | `src/prompt_enhancer/config.py:17-18,33-41,1024-1087,1193-1227`; `contracts/README.md:130-137` | Recursive read-set sentinel refusal, schema/model/path validation and stage-local observer validation exist. `kb sync` already reads all cards and the card-builder role; do not narrow it to make examples run. `kb audit` reads storage location/lock only and must not start models or read knowledge data. Add card-stage preflight both to direct K3 entry and sync loading. |
| What does K0 provide? | `src/prompt_enhancer/k0_sync.py:48-108`; `src/prompt_enhancer/k2_naming.py:291-315,402-444` | K0 returns `handoff.k2` folders/concepts/memberships and K1 image tables. Naming consumes these without publishing, preserving human declarations. Do not replace the existing handoff or implement K0-to-K5 publication. |
| What is current K1/P2 diversity evidence? | `src/prompt_enhancer/k2_observe.py:31-94,180-225,317-320`; `src/prompt_enhancer/storage/tables.py:87-95` | P2 computes cosine connected components using the current K1 vectors and returns updated `images.near_duplicate_group`. K3 consumes these stored group IDs and folder/image memberships; it must not recompute pairwise embedding distances. Observation visibility and distinct supporting image IDs, not folder multiplicity, determine support. |
| How must observation contexts be handled? | `src/prompt_enhancer/k2_observe.py:97-153,234-254,282-319`; `src/prompt_enhancer/storage/tables.py:101-107` | `context_key` distinguishes exact image-call/name sets. One image can have multiple current contexts; retain their evidence, but compute `seen_count` and each group's support as image-ID sets. Never multiply support by context/folder/repeated-row count, or silently drop conflicting context evidence. Consume current P2 rows, not historical accumulated observations; reject dangling concept/image references. |
| How are overrides parsed and hashed? | `src/prompt_enhancer/k2_naming.py:95-106,162-173,397-401,482-500`; `contracts/schemas/kb_overrides.v0.schema.json:1-19`; `contracts/README.md:124` | Existing strict YAML/schema parsing, normalized source-key collision refusal, exact-file SHA-256 and `k2_overrides` checkpoints are reusable. ### Model-written parts and model-facing schema

**Yes: part of each card is model-written.** Existing PM decision 8 requires model grouping, summary and semantic lint; code writes stability/counts and all identity/revision fields. A model may propose the existing kind enum; an override always wins. Code carries model revision in card revisions and labels observed facets `origin: generated`. Human facts/descriptions retain their separate provenance.

Proposed `card_builder_reply.v0` is a strict object (additionalProperties=false at every object level):

```text
{
  kind: null | character | clothing | place | object | style | other,
  facets: [{facet_position: integer >= 0,
            groups: [{value: string, observation_positions: [integer >= 0]}]}],
  view_groups: [{value: string, observation_positions: [integer >= 0]}],
  summary: string,
  lint_findings: [{code: string, message: string,
                   facet_positions: [integer >= 0],
                   observation_positions: [integer >= 0],
                   curated_positions: [integer >= 0]}]
}
```

All shown fields are required; `kind: null` is the explicit absent proposal. Positions are local zero-based labels, never pipeline identifiers. Code assembles canonical ordered inputs per exact stored facet, with separate observation/view/curated positions, verifies every visible input observation belongs to exactly one value group of its facet, and rejects unknown/repeated/omitted positions and malformed groups. Non-visible evidence remains available for lint but cannot supply support. View groups similarly partition the supplied view evidence; equivalent view wording is model-grouped rather than treated as diversity by string inequality. Generated facet names stay open strings from stored rows; no per-language or per-kind content list is added.

The reply contains no concept/image/folder/context/snapshot ID, group/variant ID, provenance revision, visibility count, support count or stability label. Code constructs evidence refs, deterministic group/variant IDs, counts and labels, then validates the full required-summary `concept_card.v0`. A reply with forged code-owned fields is rejected before commit. Semantic lint must cite only positions actually supplied; a schema-valid hallucinated reference is still invalid. Curated free text cannot silently become a facet value or a `curated` facet label.

System prompt bytes come solely from `prompts/card_builder_v0.txt`; all names, observation values including `text_in_image`, views and curated text enter a separately JSON-encoded user-data object. Delimiter-looking strings remain data. CPU double tests prove placement and preservation, not real-model resistance to injection or semantic grouping/lint quality (R-026/R-020).

### Ranked questions for PM approval

These are proposed answers, not accepted product decisions. Recall build/search and the stage/decision reads did not settle them. Everything else above follows the existing documents literally.

1. **How does `name` select its source and handle identity/provenance?** Decision 125 settles the key and precedence, while the existing file is concept-name keyed (`contracts/README.md:124`) and concept IDs hash names (`storage/identity.py:16-24`). **Proposed answer:** preserve the root mapping: `{old_concept_name: {name: corrected_name, ...}}` targets that known declared/generated concept in every folder membership, without folder-path selectors or physical folder renames. Apply after raw naming and before observations; preserve human `declared_names`/text, record an `override` name-source enum/report/checkpoint, apply the other keys to the replacement concept, and derive the replacement ID by the existing identity function. Refuse chained/ambiguous target renames and normalized target collisions rather than merging concepts; unmatched sources remain lint only. Removing/changing `name` reconstructs from raw K0/naming results, never from the already renamed output. If decision 125 requires selecting an individual folder (including a nameless folder) independently of the source concept, please specify that selector rather than silently changing the root-key contract.
2. **What complete five-label stability policy and numeric diversity measure should P3 implement?** Stage `K3-cards.md:14-18` defines the main cases, but not >1 visible image below `min_visible_images`, no other-facet variation, or the numeric diversity formula. **Proposed answer:** count distinct clear/partial image IDs; multiple semantic value groups -> `varies` regardless of majority; one visible image -> `single_observation`; a single group is `stable` only with the configured minimum, variation in another model-grouped facet or view, and sufficient independent near-duplicate groups. Zero-visible facets supply no asserted value and produce visibility-gap lint. Define low diversity as `distinct near_duplicate_group count / distinct visible image count <= cards.low_diversity_max_group_ratio` (required `fit_from_gt`, finite [0,1]); otherwise treat insufficient support/variation conservatively as `stable_low_diversity`, with the reason in lint. This last use broadens the table's literal near-identical case and requires explicit approval; do not quietly label it stable, single, or varies. Pin the accepted algorithm as `cards.stability_policy.version: invariance_v0`; no threshold is fitted in P3.
3. **What audit sample controls/output should the new CLI expose?** `docs/execution.md:21` and task Goal require a sample, but specify no counts/seed. **Proposed answer:** required `--folder-count`, `--card-count`, `--seed` plus existing config/overlay arguments; integer nonnegative counts (zero skips that population), explicit integer seed, cap each count at the available population, and sample sorted populations without replacement using an isolated seeded RNG. Print JSON with pinned snapshot ID, seed, requested/actual counts, model-named folders with names and provenance, and sampled full cards/facts/labels/lint/evidence refs. No model call or knowledge-root read; missing published snapshot prints an explicit empty/missing result and creates no store. No default sample size/seed or new profile key.

### Configuration audit (P3)

Profile shorthand: **F** = `configs/enhancer.example.yaml`; **D** = `configs/enhancer.dev.example.yaml`, which inherits F's cards/role values; **O** = deployment overlays, which inherit but cannot declare fitted card values or roles. **B** denotes each selected/default backend under `vlm.backends.<name>`; no new backend value was chosen. All test numbers/settings are labelled synthetic. Results are recorded in the P3 Completion report below; this table is not a claim that an unrun check passed.

Test shorthand (parameter names below identify the individual row): **CR** = `test_each_card_read_leaf_refuses_before_activity` (missing/sentinel/wrong type); **LR** = `test_loader_card_leaves_refuse_missing_and_sentinel`; **RO** = `test_card_role_override_observed`; **BO** = `test_card_backend_override_changes_cache_record`; **PO** = `test_card_policy_overrides_change_labels_and_output`; **IP** = `test_invalid_card_policies_refused`; **SO** = `test_store_configuration_overrides_are_consumed_or_refused`. These live in `tests/test_k3_config.py` except SO in `tests/test_k3_edges.py`. A singleton exception is explicit: there is no second product behavior, so supported behavior and precise invalid/missing/sentinel refusal replace an invented non-default option (P1 Q5 approval).

M1 disables multiple-value `varies`; M2 disables near-duplicate refusal; M3 inflates support counts; M4 bypasses exact value partition validation; M5 bypasses the reply schema validator; M6 omits membership identity replacement; M7 emits an empty card sample; M8 leaves audit lease directories. M1-M4/M6-M8 fail assertions; M5 fails the required exception assertion. No production module was mutated, no whole source tree copied, no collection error counted as a kill.

Retained-literal scan: scoped `git diff -U0 -- src/prompt_enhancer` additions are in `.tmp/T-010-p3/runtime-literal-diff.txt`; the final static report also inventories literals/hashes of new modules that Git diff does not yet list. Classification is the final audit row above: protocol/schema/diagnostic vocabulary and structural cardinalities, artifact stage/hash/version identities, configuration reads, and labelled test-only values. No fitted deployment number or natural-language content-matching rule was introduced.

Additional tool limitations: a read-only CIM query for a recorded check's child PID was denied by the sandbox, and the optional sleep tool was outside the exact role allowlist. Used check-session waits instead; no escalation was attempted. Recorded runner/limiter PIDs and command exit records remain the process evidence. No service or model server was started.

Discovered during the whole-suite run: `k0_checkpoint.py:163-165` retains previous concept metadata by normalized ID. A case-only/NFKC-equivalent name override can therefore leave its display spelling in a fresh K0 handoff after removal, although the freshly parsed memberships keep the original human spelling. Add the general restoration from raw naming memberships before applying overrides and a regression for ASCII case/full-width forms; this follows accepted Q1, without changing identity or human declarations. An attempted stop of the owned full-gate PID 7204 via hidden `taskkill /T` was denied (`ERROR: Access denied`, exit 1). No escalation or alternate termination method was attempted. Let that one full run finish, then run the detecting red regression and the corrected focused checks. Report the whole-suite evidence as preceding this isolated correction, rather than claim the final source hash received a second full run contrary to the PM's limit. Evidence: `.tmp/T-010-p3/full-cancellation.log`.

The same structural class audit found that multiple conflicting contexts of just one image could incorrectly supply the other-facet variation proof. Accepted Q2 requires evidence of variation, not repeated/ambiguous assignments: exclude ambiguous per-image assignments when establishing variation across images, while retaining all values in their own facet. The bounded `edge-red script .tmp/T-010-p3/edge_probe.py` command ran through the limiter without pytest fixtures or basetemp access during the full run; exit 1 printed `{"same_identity_restored": false, "label_with_only_ambiguous_single_image_variation": "stable"}`. This is an intended detecting red result, not a collection failure. Both corrections will be applied after the unchanged runtime's full run, then checked by the same probe and final focused regressions. A prompt-containment assertion also now compares decoded raw bytes, so CRLF checkouts are tested without read_text newline normalization.

Windows-held lease bytes cannot be read by a peer test; use name/stat preservation and continuing pin use. A mutant expecting an exception can be killed by pytest's `DID NOT RAISE`, not just an AssertionError string; retain the failure trace and exclude collection failures. Normalized identity can retain stale display metadata across K0 merges, and repeated contexts can fake diversity unless the proof is per image; both now have detecting regressions. Confirm process-control permissions early if planning to interrupt a long check; when denied, preserve the check result and report its source boundary.

#### Key dependency audit (P1, P2, P3 and audit)

These are the intended dependencies of the model result or stored artifact, not a hash of every validated setting. Transport/resource settings continue to be validated/consumed even when excluded from reuse identity. Old referenced artifacts stay immutable; changing the key definition can cause a one-time safe miss, never reinterpret an old key as a new format.

| Key / source | Fields the model or stored result depends on; retain | Remove from semantic reuse; invariant test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Common semantic model/prompt/schema revisions; P1 `:240`, P2 `:133`, P3 `:234` | Role's configured inference/validation intent (`max_tokens`, sampling, reasoning, output), selected provider/resolved engine/API version, configured model/processor selection and pinned revisions, backend `extra_body`; applicable fallback model identity only when routing permits it. Prompt ID/content and role reply/configured schemas. Image roles additionally resolve their effective max side/format/quality/alpha handling and processor revision; text-only assessment/cards have no image-processor dependency. | Whole backend/role/vision dictionaries, unrelated backend/default when unreachable, addresses/credentials, HTTP deadlines/retries/backoff, probe budgets, request budgets, worker/resource/lifecycle/report settings and unused inherited image leaves. Preserve deliberate model/artifact selection and routing-policy effects. Parameterize operational-only changes versus real semantic model/processor changes across naming, observation and grouping, with no client activity on reuse. |
| P1 `k2_draw_head`, `k2_draw`, `k2_redraw`; `k2_naming.py:318-374` | Head: folder identity and sampling-policy namespace. Draw: namespace, sorted distinct candidate content IDs and explicit ambiguous recovery context; saved ordered sample is the result. Redraw: exact recovery/change event and involved keys. These artifacts intentionally describe population/recovery, not a model reply. | No global overrides/model config/operational leaves belong here. Preserve raw committed draws, quota/change/missing-sample/recovery semantics and event reporting. Test unrelated override/backend edits leave draw keys/sample unchanged. |
| P1 `k2_assessment`; `:519-529` | Exact assessor JSON payload: basename hint, raw info text, declared name positions and assignment requirement; prompt/model/schema revision. | Full folder path is not sent to the model. Remove redundant folder-scoped envelope identity from reusable raw semantic replies; reconstruct current folder trace at application/report time. Unrelated folder/override/operational edits preserve reply; actual text/name/assignment/prompt changes invalidate. |
| P1 `k2_naming`; `:555-570` | Exact namer payload, ordered selected image content IDs (image labels/order matter), effective image processor, prompt/model/schema revision. | Candidate population/draw artifact key and full folder path must not force a new call when actual payload and ordered sample are unchanged. Keep population/recovery in draw artifacts, current folder association in applied rows/report. Test an unrelated population/draw change with unchanged attachments and an unrelated override change, alongside true sampled-image/order/name changes. |
| P1 `k2_overrides`, `k2_names_applied`, call-failure diagnostics; `:506-510,596-607`, `:487-496` | Raw override artifact intentionally retains exact file hash and parsed data. Applied artifact retains exact raw provenance, resulting rows, rename/lint trace. Diagnostics retain failure status/call-record provenance for the semantic call key. | Do not strip exact hashes from provenance artifacts. A comment edit can create a new raw/applied provenance artifact but cannot force a new assessment/naming/grouping call. Assert both sides of this distinction, including removal/rebuild. |
| P2 grouping identity, observation context and bundle; `k2_observe.py:31-78,133-147,193-224,242-255` | Near-duplicate components depend on current eligible vectors, distance and embedder identity; group ID identifies algorithm/revision/member set. Observation key retains image content ID, sorted distinct effective concept names, effective model/processor/prompt/reply/stored-schema/facet revision; bundle retains declared identities, reply and derived current document/context. | Folder path, description/curation/override-file hash, unrelated concept, near-duplicate quota/distance and operational leaves do not change the observation reply for an identical image/name set. Quota/distance still control scheduling/grouping. Extend properties/effect pairs for each exclusion and retain true image/name/schema/processor invalidation. |
| P3 `k3_grouping`; `k3_cards.py:253-258,265-288` | Exact ID-free local-position payload: concept name, facet labels, value/visibility/view rows, curated text/origin and suppressed facets; semantic model/prompt/reply-schema revisions. Bundle validates key, payload, reply and call record. | Whole overrides and file hash, other concepts, alias/type-only metadata, raw observation provenance/IDs when the positional payload is unchanged, curated folder provenance and snapshot/policy/report-only changes. Position order stays bound to payload; map reused positions against current rows at assembly. Tests distinguish reuse from current evidence-ref/document rebuilding; update obsolete context-only invalidation assertions. |
| P3 `k3_card` document; `:290-307` | Derived card content including current counts/IDs/curated provenance/findings/kind/revisions and target snapshot, stored-card schema, implemented assembly/policy identity. Keep policy inputs actually used for labels/variants, excluding report-only behavior. | Whole `cards` dictionary, `lint_report`, irrelevant label ordering, unrelated overrides and redundant grouping-input metadata cannot invalidate an identical stored result. Assert new snapshot/current evidence/policy outputs rebuild documents while grouping is reused; report toggle alone does not change card bytes/key. |
| P3 failure diagnostic; `:313-319` | Semantic failed-call key, actual error and available call-record trace. This is diagnostic evidence, not reusable successful model work. | Never let its write or failure reporting demote a done grouping record. Test successful grouping survives all caught post-commit failure sites and is reused on retry. |

Output depends on the pinned snapshot, each sorted eligible population, its requested/capped count, integer seed and population RNG label. Folder sample also includes only its current memberships. | Neither population consumes the other's random stream. Unrelated profile/source/model settings are outside the audit read set; each count is independent and zero skips that read. Test both directions, source exclusions, sorting/permutation and caller-RNG isolation while preserving pin/lease/no-source-read guarantees. |

#### Ranked questions (one; proposed answer)

1. **What existing-ID assignments does F5 authorize?** The review's reproduced defect is `Alice` -> `ALICE` for an already-member folder (`probe1.py`, P1): the same identity should retain its raw canonical display for every other folder, with a spelling lint. The dispatch also says an existing-ID selector "adds that folder's membership", which could authorize a nameless folder or a different raw concept to join an occupied concept; decision 131 and the accepted refusal tests currently forbid those normalized target collisions. **Proposed conservative answer:** fix same-source/same-ID spelling and membership locality now; retain distinct-source/nameless-to-occupied target refusals unless the PM explicitly confirms that the newer instruction authorizes deliberate membership assignment into an existing concept. If it does, preserve existing target display/kind and all other memberships, change only the selected folder, lint the differing spelling, and retain conflicting-name/new-target/global-rename collision refusals. No physical folder merge or concept-definition merge is implied.

Audit notes: explicit supported singletons use the previously approved supported/refused behavior exception, not a fabricated alternate value. Retained configured inference intent is intentionally distinct from actual mapped-body identity under decision 119. The self-facet inequality filter was removed as mathematically redundant: a single value group cannot prove across-group variation, and multiple values force `varies` before stability. Supporting-image, visibility, ambiguous-assignment and suppression filters each have discriminating tests. Source model selection remains pinned/validated; no model artifact was loaded or measured.

### P3 fix round 2

#### PM answers (P3 fix round 2)

Implementation approved by `P3 fix round 2 Step 0 approved; implement it`. These choices are conservative, reversible **PM decisions pending user review**.

1. The original main-suite assertion text is unavailable: only its last three output lines were retained. Proceed with deterministic class regressions and key/state/reference capture; report the original intermittent trigger as unconfirmed unless captured. PM's next main-suite run will retain full output.
2. Put one done-preservation rule at the `storage/store.py` checkpoint transition boundary for fail and queue, under the writer mutex, reading current durable state across helpers/runs. Remove K3's stage guard. Keep `done()`/`reusable()` skip semantics exactly, preserve item-error reports, and run every existing storage and K1 test unchanged; identify any necessary test expectation change precisely.
3. Keep `knowledge.concept_separator` out of raw reply identity. Always validate current settings, report refusal without a fresh call, retain the raw checkpoint, and reuse after reverting. P2's done-to-queued path is in scope.
4. Execute the four approved mutants (including queue-only preservation bypass), then 30 named-test invocations, two combined K2/K3 selections and one exact focused gate. Stop/report if the same step fails twice. No Git/index writes, network/installs/live models; one fixed basetemp removed per run and hidden Windows children. List every new file for PM staging.

#### Step 0 (approved plan; research before implementation)

**Goal / scope:** close the completed-checkpoint demotion class across P1 naming, P2 observation and K3 (R-007, R-010, R-014, R-015, R-017, R-018, R-026), and investigate the intermittent referenced-reply regression. The deliverable of this turn is this Step 0 only. Proposed implementation scope after approval: the shared checkpoint transition boundary in `src/prompt_enhancer/storage/store.py`, minimal removal of the misleading K3 failure guard, associated K2/K3/storage regressions and relevant checkpoint/stage documentation. K1 is a read/compatibility-test consumer of that shared boundary; no embedding algorithm change is proposed. Keep semantic/request keys, model-facing payloads, artifact immutability and occupied-target policy unchanged. Reviewer P3-1 curated ordering is outside this one-class round.

**May assume:** tiny labelled doubles, explicit distinct test session IDs, key-based checkpoint assertions and controlled test timestamps; the PM's dispatched scope/acceptance criteria. **Must ask:** a product/contract change, any expansion beyond this demotion class, and missing original failure evidence before claiming its precise cause. The proposed choices below are conservative, reversible **PM decisions pending user review**, not newly user-confirmed decisions. Applying the existing `config-audit` and `handoff-report` skills.

**Research evidence / what is established:**

- Source-verified: `Checkpoints.done` deliberately filters other-run rows when resume is false (`storage/store.py:1076-1094`); `reusable` additionally requires live references and intact bytes (`:1097-1112`). Those functions answer whether work may be skipped, not whether an earlier completion may be overwritten by a failure. `state` does not filter runs (`:1071-1073`). `fail` currently writes failed unconditionally (`:1153-1157`); `queue` also overwrites unconditionally (`:1146-1149`). Both use the existing writer mutex/live-session decorator (`:710-720`).
- Source-verified: Sonnet's two probes were read in full, `.tmp/T-010/retest1-p3/probes/test_probe_k3.py:1-29` and `test_probe_p1.py:1-26`, with the report's P3-2 at `review.md:65-76`. K3 resume-off invalid recomputation can overwrite a prior unreferenced done row; P1 cached-reply validation can overwrite a done naming row on a separator change. Their printed outputs are reviewer evidence, not worker-run experiments in this turn.
- Source-verified: the P2 selected-image failure path is also unsafe for an earlier unreferenced done row when resume is off (`k2_observe.py:232-233,260-288`), despite committing outside that catch (`:292-293`). The unselected-image queue path (`:255-258`) can first replace that same done row with queued. Protecting failure alone would leave a route around the invariant.
- Source-verified: writer run IDs are UUID4 values (`storage/store.py:749`), not clock-derived. Checkpoint rows are reduced by exact `(stage, input_key)` (`:793-806`); sync-state segment names use `time.time_ns`, a process sequence and UUID suffix (`:104-105`), read in sorted name order (`:787-791`, `_children` at `:337`). A wall-clock reversal is a possible ordering hazard to discriminate, not an established cause of this failure. `recorded_at` comes from the store clock (`:1059`), and is not the latest-row comparison key.
- Source-verified: `test_referenced_reply_survives_caught_failure_with_resume_disabled` (`tests/test_k3_fix_round1.py:401-420`) uses `checkpoint_rows(...)[0]` at line 418 without asserting the exact key or row count. Its current fixture creates one concept (`tests/k3_fixtures.py:63-80`) and `checkpoint_rows` returns unsorted values (`:99-101`); ordering is an assertion weakness, not proof it caused the observed failure. `project_root` is session-shared while `k2_env`/its store are function-scoped (`tests/conftest.py:57-70`); schemas/prompts therefore also need discrimination when investigating suite-order effects. The failed assertion text is unavailable, so the exact original intermittent trigger is **unconfirmed**. Neither UUID timing nor arbitrary row order will be asserted as its cause without captured evidence.
- Additional shared callers read: K1 image source/embedding failures and text embedding failures at `k1_embed.py:394,411,474`; each already reports the current item failure separately. Production search found no other checkpoint fail callers, and the sole queue caller is P2. Storage lifecycle/path guard tests call fail/queue on independent keys (`tests/test_storage_review_r1.py:288-294`, `test_storage_review_r3.py:288-293,381-385`, `test_storage_review_r4.py:224-238`, `test_storage_snapshots.py:138-142`). These consumers must be included in compatibility checks if the shared storage boundary changes.
- Read-only integration comparison completed: `git diff 0c93c6d f74237c -- src pyproject.toml`. Main adds `_os_support.os_supports` (native capability checks by function name), uses it in K0 POSIX scanning, hardens POSIX ancestor opening in `storage/_anchored.py:216-239`, renames the Windows root helper to avoid a name collision (`:457,553` in the diff), and adds test dependency `markdown-it-py==4.2.0`. It changes no checkpoint table, state transition, segment/run ID generation or K2/K3 function. Anchored storage I/O is an indirect dependency of the failing path; its POSIX behavior changes, while the Windows helper rename preserves the call. The diff does not establish the intermittent cause. No integration/rebase/cherry-pick/index operation was performed.
- Recall before deciding: build returned `indexed=0 unchanged=92 removed=0 refused=0 chunks=0 skipped_chunks=0`; search `checkpoint done fail resume run_id separator` returned no matches. Read the decisions index's decision 119 and the lessons digest. No model/network/library behavior is inferred from external summaries; this round uses repository-owned checkpoint/parser code.

#### Shared fix and validation-setting decision

**Confirmed root cause:** completion eligibility and completion preservation were conflated. `done()`/`reusable()` are intentionally conditional read/skip APIs. A later unsuccessful attempt is an item error, and cannot erase a committed raw reply merely because the current run declines to skip it. The old F6 closure covered only the K3 in-run/referenced cases; it missed P1/P2 and prior-run unreferenced completions.

**Proposed shared rule:** enforce completed-state preservation at the existing checkpoint nonterminal transition boundary under the writer mutex, common to `fail` and `queue`. A key committed/reused by this run, or durably done in any run, keeps its done row and artifact; attempts to fail/queue it make no checkpoint transition. Missing/queued/failed keys still receive the requested transition. Read durable current state across helpers/sessions, rather than trusting a stale `Checkpoints._rows` view or calling run-filtered `done()`/reference-filtered `reusable()`. Include two handles for the same stage/key in the regression so a stale helper cannot demote another helper's commit. Keep successful recomputation/commit, referenced-byte immutability, checksum validation, writer lifetime/locking and corruption refusal unchanged. P1/P2/K3 continue recording current errors in their existing reports; remove the misleading stage-specific K3 eligibility-as-preservation check in favor of the shared rule. No broad exception catch or historical-state repair is proposed.

**Separator / similar settings:** keep `knowledge.concept_separator` out of raw naming reply identity. It is a local application/validation rule, not part of the exact namer payload; changing it can make the same raw reply unacceptable without changing model-visible input. Always validate against the current separator before applying any cached or fresh reply; report a current folder error, leave the earlier raw checkpoint intact, and reuse it again when the original separator is restored. Do not apply an invalid reply or silently fall back to a fresh model call just to evade that refusal. Schema contents/revisions and actual context/name positions are already in the key (`k2_naming.py:259-278`, `:548-559`); they continue invalidating when semantic input/contract changes. P2 reply/stored/configured schema and facet version are retained (`k2_observe.py:133-143`), K3 reply/configured schemas are retained (`k3_cards.py:240-244`); card policy/derived-card validation remains downstream of raw grouping (`:294-310`) and must preserve that reply on failure. Test current validation refusal and revert reuse, rather than adding broad configuration to a semantic key. Decision-119 effective/request hashes remain untouched.

#### Determinism investigation and regression design

The original missing assertion cannot be reconstructed from pass counts. After approval, first capture exact key, durable state, output hash, references and writer/session IDs at each boundary in the original regression and the two supplied probe scenarios. Discriminate state demotion from report failure, extra request, row selection, artifact change and current-schema refusal. Assert a map keyed by the exact grouping artifact input key, with explicit population size; seed/add unrelated stage keys in differing insertion orders so `[0]` can never decide the result. Exercise explicitly distinct same/other-run IDs and reference presence without retries/sleeps. Control test clocks with labelled doubles and test equal-time segment names through the real sequence suffix; investigate any reproduced ordering problem before proposing a storage-order change. Do not freeze a production bug out of the test or widen its expected result.

Once fixed, run the original named test in **30 independent limiter-wrapped pytest invocations**, each with the same fixed basetemp deleted afterward; each failure is evidence, not a retry to obtain a pass. Also run all `tests/test_k2_*.py` and `test_k3_*.py` together **twice**, then the exact previous focused file selection **once**. Keep every original assertion's meaning and add failure diagnostics. If the original intermittent trigger remains unidentified despite deterministic class reproductions, say so explicitly and hand the captured evidence to PM; 30 green runs alone do not establish a cause.

### P3 fix round 3

#### PM answers (P3 fix round 3)

Approved by `P3 fix round 3 Step 0 approved; implement it as planned`. All choices below are conservative, reversible **PM decisions pending user review**.

1. Q1 yes: memoize one helper per stage per Writer. The writer view is authoritative and separate from helper-local dictionaries; clear the memo on rewrite, compaction, GC and every close. Tests needing independent/stale helpers construct `Checkpoints(writer, stage)` directly with an explanatory comment. Stop if a src caller requires distinct helper identity; the reread source audit, including K0 consumers, found none.
2. Implement the lazy writer view, post-success append updates, ambiguous/out-of-order invalidation, rewrite failure-path invalidation and separate GC working copy. Preserve all existing done/reusable/load integrity checks.
3. Structural bounds: zero state reads from an empty log and at most S reads after reopening with S seeded segments; no timing threshold.
4. Implement independent fresh-merge properties, failed-append/rewrite controls, and K3 failed-state/error coverage for never-done and already queued/failed keys.
5. Run the five accepted mutants and focused gates, then one full `tests/` gate through the worktree limiter with full output retained under `.tmp/T-010-p3/`.
6. Conditional reference-cache rebuild cost under interleaved reference additions is outside scope, recorded as a PM follow-up under Noticed, not done. Follow the literal conservative reading for routine ambiguity, record it here and continue; stop only for a requirement change or a conflict with a recorded decision.

**Implementation plan status:** all four planned steps complete; REVIEWING, ready for independent PM review. The completed Step 0 Configuration impact below gated runtime editing (R-017, R-015, R-007, R-010). The config-audit and handoff-report skills were applied.

#### Step 0 (plan only; awaiting PM approval)

**Goal / R-IDs:** remove repeated whole-log checkpoint reads without weakening durable completion, resume, artifact-integrity or current-error reporting guarantees (R-017, R-015, R-007, R-010). Close Sonnet retest-2 P1-1 and the P3-1 K3 failure-state test gap. This turn delivers measured research and this plan only; runtime implementation requires the PM's second message.

**Scope after approval:** `src/prompt_enhancer/storage/store.py`, `tests/test_storage_checkpoint_preservation.py`, `tests/test_k3_fix_round2.py`, and this task section. Add a small focused storage regression file only if separation materially improves readability. K1/K2/K3 callers are read/test consumers; no stage algorithm, semantic key, model request, configuration profile, persisted schema or checkpoint filename change is proposed. Reference-cache redesign, historical state repair, tooling and PM-owned files are outside scope.

**May assume:** the existing single-writer protocol, tiny synthetic stores, labelled transparent counting/failure doubles, and the previously accepted fix-round-2 preservation rule. **Must ask:** approval before implementation, helper-factory identity choice below, or any product/contract/permission change. The worker does not create subagents, touch the staged index, or infer authorization for live runs.

**Unknowns / failure modes:** a cache can become stale after successful append, publish a row before a failed append, retain removed keys after GC/rewrite, alias GC's mutable working dictionary, or suppress validation when initialized from disk. Merely caching the merged dictionary leaves a full Python dictionary scan in every helper constructor. Segment-name ordering, including equal timestamps, must match fresh merging. A warm checkpoint view must never become an artifact-integrity cache. Existing wall-clock reversal exposure must not become a new cache-versus-disk disagreement. The original fix-round-2 intermittent trigger remains unconfirmed; this round makes no new causal claim about it.

#### Research evidence and measured class audit

**Read raw / source-verified:** prior `### P3 fix round 2` through its Handoff (`:2289-2450` before this insertion); `.tmp/T-010/retest2-p3/review.md:15-34,44-50,61-77`, all three supplied probes and `mutate.py`; PM's `.tmp/T-010/p3-fix2.md` and `p3-fix2-approve.md`. Read the Writer state methods, GC and reference paths, the entire Checkpoints class, SnapshotBuilder reference lifecycle, and their K1/K2/K3 consumers. Decisions 119/125 and the lessons digest were consulted. `recall.py build` returned exactly `indexed=0 unchanged=92 removed=0 refused=0 chunks=0 skipped_chunks=0`; `recall.py search 'checkpoint sync_state cache quadratic'` returned no matches. No network research is needed: the changed algorithm is repository-owned, and no external API change is proposed.

**Confirmed root cause:** Writer has a session reference cache (`store.py:738,982-1001`) but no session checkpoint view. `_state_rows` lists, reads, validates and reduces all state segments on every invocation (`:793-806`). `_transition` invokes it per fail/queue (`:1157`), and Checkpoints construction invokes it again (`:1069`). Thus both the new transition path and older per-item helper construction perform quadratic segment reads as the append-only log grows. `_referenced` is a separate, already cached path; its manifests are parsed through `pq.read_table(pa.BufferReader(data))` (`:491-505`), so a read_table_file counter alone cannot measure manifest reads.

**Experiment (CPU, synthetic fixtures; not a live throughput claim):** one bounded pytest command ran N=50 and N=200 on unchanged runtime. A labelled transparent wrapper around `storage.store.read_table_file` classified state versus other table reads and always invoked the original function. A second transparent wrapper counted `_manifest` calls, which read/parse real manifest bytes independently of read_table_file. No return values, writes, locks or transition behavior were replaced. The constructor experiment commits one unique key then constructs a helper after each commit; the transition experiments reuse one helper and use unique keys. Counters reset at each phase, exclude setup and writer teardown, and include neither asserted fresh-oracle reads nor retries. Reference experiments use one real validated synthetic snapshot and, for invalidation, one growing unfinished builder.

| Measured phase | N=50 actual reads | N=200 actual reads | Verdict in items / interpretation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| N queue calls, initially empty log | 1,225 state | 19,900 state | quadratic, exactly N(N-1)/2 |
| N fail calls, initially empty log | 1,225 state | 19,900 state | quadratic, exactly N(N-1)/2 |
| Helper after each of N commits | 1,275 state | 20,100 state | quadratic, exactly N(N+1)/2; predates fix round 2 |
| One compaction after those queue calls; independently after fail calls | 50 state each | 200 state each | linear per compaction, not per item in the current stages |
| One GC after N commits, zero retention | 50 state + 2 registry | 200 state + 2 registry | linear per GC; removes unreferenced outputs and rewrites state |
| N reusable calls with stable reference topology | 1 registry + 1 manifest total | 1 registry + 1 manifest total | constant file reads after one build; artifact checks still run separately |
| N commits with warm stable reference topology | 0 table / manifest | 0 table / manifest | constant reference-table reads; artifact writes are excluded from this metric |
| N commits interleaved with reference_artifacts additions | 49 registry + 49 manifest | 199 registry + 199 manifest | linear file reads for one live snapshot; each addition invalidates the reference cache, except after the last call no later lookup occurs |

The last row is not proof that all reference work is linear: rebuilding traverses the growing builder entries, and S live snapshots require S manifest reads per invalidation. Repeated invalidation can therefore cost quadratic CPU in growing entries, or quadratic manifest reads if S also grows with N. No production call to `reference_artifacts(...)` was found outside its definition in `src`; this conditional cost is recorded, not silently included in the checkpoint fix or claimed solved.

**Every matching call in src, excluding definitions/comments (line numbers before this round):**

| Callee and direct caller | Frequency / stage consumers | Current verdict |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `_state_segments`, `_state_rows` at `store.py:796` | Once per full reduction; reached per transition and per helper below | linear per invocation in segments; quadratic across per-item callers |
| `_state_segments`, `_rewrite_state` at `:815` | One listing per rewrite; GC and compaction | linear per run in the current callers; listing itself makes zero table reads |
| `_state_segments`, `_compact_state` at `:822` | One listing per normal Writer exit (`:755-762`); also explicitly callable | linear per run; no table reads from listing itself |
| `_state_rows`, `_compact_state` at `:823` | Once only when more than one segment exists | linear per compaction; measured 50/200 reads |
| `_state_rows`, `_gc_artifacts` at `:1019` | Once per `gc` (`:978-979`); mutates its returned working dictionary before rewrite | linear per GC; measured 50/200 state reads |
| `_state_rows`, `Checkpoints.__init__` at `:1069` | Once per helper construction via `Writer.checkpoints` (`:825-827`) | quadratic for per-item helper construction, measured 1,275/20,100 reads |
| `_state_rows`, `Checkpoints._transition` at `:1157` | Once per fail/queue attempt, including attempts skipped because already done | quadratic with a growing log; measured 1,225/19,900 for both non-skipped paths |
| `_referenced`, `_gc_artifacts` at `:1018` | Once per GC, after reference-cache invalidation at `:978` | linear in live manifests + builder entries per GC |
| `_referenced`, `Checkpoints.reusable` at `:1103` | At most once per candidate done key; K1/K2/K3 per-item consumers | cached, constant file reads across N stable lookups (1 registry + 1 manifest in probe) |
| `_referenced`, `Checkpoints.commit.immutable` at `:1123` | Once during each artifact commit before publication | cached, zero warm reference reads; conditional rebuild cost after reference changes as measured above |

**Consumer frequency / source-verified:** K1 image/text stages construct one helper each (`k1_embed.py:376,457`), then fail per affected item (`:394,411,474`). K2 observation constructs one helper (`k2_observe.py:227`) and queues each deferred image (`:257`) or fails selected work (`:286`). K3 constructs grouping/card helpers once (`k3_cards.py:251-252`) but creates a diagnostic helper per caught recorded failure (`:320`), then calls grouping.fail (`:323`). K2 naming constructs helpers per folder/draw/replay/evaluation/failure (`k2_naming.py:349,359,383,507,516,522,583,587,616`), plus per-run override/applied helpers (`:534,633`). Its `_load` (`:241`) and recovery (`:318`) reach reusable; `_save` (`:253`) reaches commit. K1 `_skip_or_reuse` (`:322`) and `_commit_record` (`:329`), K2 observation (`:233,291`), and K3 (`:265,291,305,309,320`) reach the same cached reference boundary. The source search found no other calls of the three audited private methods. GC/compaction called once per item by a hypothetical external consumer would repeatedly rescan; current production stage callers do not do that.

#### Proposed root fix and invariants

1. **One writer-owned authoritative checkpoint view:** lazily build latest `(stage, input_key) -> normalized row` once from the existing sorted, schema/path-validated segment merge while holding the live Writer mutex. Keep an explicitly fresh merge path for the property oracle and disk reconciliation; do not redefine the property to compare the cache with itself. Give `_transition` a point lookup into the writer view, independent of its helper's `_rows`, run filter or artifact skip eligibility. Cached mutable dictionaries are private; `_gc_artifacts` receives a separate working dictionary, never the authoritative cache itself.
2. **Successful append is the update boundary:** build/validate the sync_state table as today, publish the segment atomically, and only after `_write_table` succeeds update already-built cache entries from that table's normalized rows. A failed pre-publication write must leave the prior view untouched; ambiguous/post-publication failures invalidate rather than invent a durable result. A cold cache may remain cold until first read. Preserve filename precedence, not recorded_at: equal timestamps still follow the real sequence suffix. If a new filename precedes the view's last segment (for example clock reversal), invalidate/rebuild using the existing sorted merge rather than applying an inconsistent append-order overwrite. No new clock policy, tuning threshold or filename format.
3. **Rewrite / compaction / GC:** `_rewrite_state` invalidates before its replacement append/deletions and in a finally path, covering empty rewrites, partial failures and keys removed by GC. Rebuild only from the segments actually left on disk on the next lookup. Compaction and GC use this same boundary; no per-site cache patch. Drop the view and helper memo on Writer close, including exceptional close. GC's deletion of done rows for collected artifacts must be observable in the next writer lookup; a successful rewrite must not retain absent keys.
4. **Avoid helper-construction scans as well:** proposed minimal factory change, subject to Q1: memoize one Checkpoints helper per stage for a live Writer; construct it from the writer view once per stage rather than scanning all segments per item. Pipeline stage count is fixed, so per-folder calls stop rebuilding whole-stage dictionaries. Retain helper `_rows` as a separate local view, not an alias into the authoritative cache; explicitly construct a second independent helper in stale-helper regressions so helper reuse cannot make those tests vacuous. A rewrite clears factory memoization as well as the writer view. No new public option or third-party abstraction.
5. **Lock / behavioral proof:** Writer acquires WRITER before recovery and holds it until after compaction (`store.py:740-762`); writer-side helpers serialize through `_writer_operation` (`:710-720`). `FileLock` uses `msvcrt.LK_NBLCK` on Windows and `fcntl.LOCK_EX | LOCK_NB` on POSIX (`storage/_fs.py:142-173`), and an anchored lock-file opener (`storage/_anchored.py:135-139`). Thus another compliant Writer cannot mutate these segments during this session; reader leases/holds do not write sync_state. This does not assume protection from a process bypassing the storage protocol. Preserve done from any run despite resume=false or a stale independent helper, exact stage/key isolation, and equal-time order. Keep done/reusable run/reference filters and fresh size/hash checks; cached done with damaged output is not reusable, and load still raises. Keep the stage item errors and deferred-work reports. The writer cache is session-scoped O(K) state, not a configurable external cache.

**Rejected narrower fixes:** cache only `_transition` (misses helper construction); cache only the helper dictionary (stale helpers/other runs can demote completion); return an authoritative mutable dictionary to GC (pre-write mutation and retained deleted keys); add periodic compaction thresholds (new deployment decisions and repeated work); optimize reference topology without a demonstrated current stage caller (different class).

#### Tests and exact performance bounds after approval

- **Read-count regression:** parameterize N=50/200, queue/fail, same-helper and per-item-factory paths, resume true/false and parquet/arrow. From an empty log, assert **zero sync_state read_table_file calls** over the measured helper/transition loop, excluding setup/teardown. Seed S=3 valid segments and reopen a Writer; across helper creation and the entire measured loop assert **at most S=3 state reads total**, with at most one initial segment listing for the lazy view, excluding fresh-oracle reads and explicit rewrites. These are structural bounds, not timing thresholds; current measured results fail them. Assert factory helper construction once per used stage under Q1, so zero file reads cannot hide repeated Python projection. Explicit compaction/rewrite is a separate phase and may rebuild from the single resulting segment once; no bound is weakened to O(N) reads per call.
- **Independent property:** extend the checkpoint sequence property with generated commit/fail/queue/compaction/GC operations, multiple stages and keys, both formats and resume values. After every successful operation assert the entire writer view equals a fresh schema/path-validated merge of physical segments, including full metadata, call records and GC marks; use a separate expected transition model for state/error invariants. Include fixed discriminating sequences where zero-retention GC removes a done artifact/key, GC retains a referenced artifact, rewrite is empty, and compaction is followed by another operation. Test failed append, failed rewrite cleanup and controlled equal/out-of-order segment names explicitly. The fresh oracle must bypass the cache, so missing invalidation cannot pass by aliasing. Bound sequence sizes/examples as test-only values and delete generated stores within the fixed basetemp.
- **Preservation and corruption:** retain and run prior cross-run/stale-helper/key-stage/unfinished-work tests. Add an explicitly distinct helper instance if factory memoization changes how tests create a stale helper. Assert done is preserved with both resume settings, no bad artifact is skipped/reused, load raises, and current errors still reach stage reports. Successful replacement commits remain allowed subject to reference immutability.
- **P3-1 K3:** in `tests/test_k3_fix_round2.py`, start with no grouping completion; return an invalid first reply through labelled CardServer so facet coverage validation raises. Assert the exact grouping key, sole matching row, `state == 'failed'`, `error == report['failed'][concept_id]` with the concrete coverage error, no output artifact, and no built card. A second case first creates queued/failed state and then fails again, killing a fail-call guard restricted to never-seen keys. Never use arbitrary checkpoint_rows(...)[0] ordering to select the assertion target.
- **Configuration / compatibility gates:** run `test_storage_config.py::test_resume_override_and_refusal` and `::test_table_format_override_and_refusal`, the new full resume/format matrix and property, all storage/K1/K2/K3 tests and `test_kb_audit.py`; then one full pipeline `tests/` gate, required by this brief. Existing missing-field/sentinel/type/read-set refusal tests stay active. No tool-suite or extra broad repetition is proposed for this storage-only change. Run lint / `ruff format --check` if available, otherwise report exact unavailable output without installing.

#### Ranked questions (one; proposed answer)
